Shipped content that carried the shape before the gate is counted in `packages/content/time-framing-baseline.json` (142 error-tier lines at recording: corpus 123, consequence cards 7, resolution sequences 6, pilot 4, reference set 2; grown once to 161, corpus 140 and reference set 4, when the report shapes were added, each added line read), and `scenario-audit-time-baseline.test.ts` fails on any line not listed and on any listed line since rewritten, so the count only falls. See `CONTENT_DOCTRINE.md` §10.1. The per-passage language and world-neutrality runners live in `passage-audit.ts` and are shared: register, British spellings, figures written as numerals, filler, real news outlets, real nation names, charter institutions, cabinet titles, and capability figures are enforced identically on scenario and consequence prose. `scenario-audit.ts` adds the scenario-only rules; `consequence-audit.ts` adds `consequence-unknown-placeholder`, `unknown-risk-id` (validated against the engine's `RISK_IDS`), `risk-affinity-off-failure`, `inert-consequence`, `costless-upside` (a success or partial card that carries no cost, no `sets` flag and nothing remembered — pure gain with no forward hook, the aftermath layer's twin of the scenario audit's `costless-position`), `valence-magnitude-inversion`, and the corpus-level `consequence-flag-never-set`, `duplicate-consequence-id`, `template-overlap`, and the coverage rules over the 45-cell instrument × rung band × valence matrix (five instruments, since `assistance` joined the ladder) (`coverage-cell-empty`, `coverage-cell-gated`, `coverage-cell-thin`). The magnitude bound (`CONSEQUENCE_FAILURE_HARM_FLOOR`) and coverage depth (`CONSEQUENCE_COVERAGE_TARGET`) are exported from `audit-lexicon.ts` so the generation brief quotes what the validator enforces rather than a remembered copy of it.

**Six rules were added from driving the rendered loops rather than from reading the schema**, and their vocabularies live in `content/src/scenario-vocabulary.ts` (full descriptions in `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s rule-family table):

| Rule | Severity | What it refuses |
|---|---|---|
| `locus-ground-mismatch` | error | A cast scenario declaring `locus.ground: "home"` beside a descriptor standing on the counterpart's ground. `resolveLocus` already defaults a cast scenario onto the counterpart's geography and a `cast: "none"` scenario onto the player's own, so the default is always right; the shipped defect was an authored override that datelined the player's own capital for a hostage week held under occupation. Leave `ground` unset unless the event is genuinely on the player's soil. |
| `parliamentary-vocabulary` | error | `minister` / `ministry` / `prime minister` / `premier` / `parliament` / `parliamentary` / `no-confidence` / `backbencher` said about **the player's own** government. One seat is playable and it is a presidency. The finding names the replacement (Secretary of …, the Department of …, Congress / the House / the Senate, impeachment / censure / a resolution, members of your own party). A `transmission` is exempt whole and so is any sentence plainly about the counterpart; the bare `minister` family on a `cast: "none"` scenario is left to `cabinet-title-literal`, and the seat/budget shorthand ("the chair", "the line") stays with `insider-shorthand` at warning. |
| `financial-basis-without-band` | error | `impact.financial.basis` with no `scale`, or `scale: "none"`: the basis says how much, the band how it is authorized, and a basis needs both. |
| `financial-basis-off-band` | warning | A stated basis more than ten times above or below its band's anchor at the week's severity (`BASIS_BAND_TOLERANCE`); the message names the band whose anchor is nearest. |
| `financial-scale-exceeds-severity` | error | `impact.financial.scale` louder than the week: routine at most `supplemental`, serious at most `emergency`, critical anything; `horizon: "permanent"` is refused on routine. `SEVERITY_SCALE_CEILING` is exported so the generation brief quotes it. |
| `instrument-reply-contradicts-rung` | error / warning | An `instrument` reply whose text names another rung's act and none of its own (error), or names no rung's act at all (warning). Rung labels come from `@admin/engine`'s `rungsForInstrument`, re-exported as `INSTRUMENT_RUNG_NAMES` so the brief prints the live ladder. |
| `press-missing-on-heavy-reply` | warning | A `critical` scenario with a heavy position answer and no `press` headline anywhere in the set. |
| `cabinet-gate-unused` | warning (pool-scale) | A batch of `CABINET_GATE_BATCH_FLOOR` (8) or more where under `CABINET_GATE_SHARE_FLOOR` (10%) of scenarios carry a `cabinetAttribute` condition. |

**The placeholder resolver reads `identity.definiteArticle`.** `renderText` (`content/src/scenario-cast.ts`) resolves `{{nation}}` and `{{counterpart}}` through `nationPhrase` (`content/src/nation-article.ts`), which supplies the article, capitalizes it where the token opens a sentence, and swallows a trailing `'s` so an article name's possessive is written the way a newsroom writes it ("the United States'", never "the United States's"). Names with no article keep exactly the apostrophe the writer typed. The field is read structurally with a name-shape fallback, so the resolver was correct before the dataset carried the flag and defers to it now that it does (see §1's `identity.definiteArticle`).

## 8. What this doc does not yet cover

`EffectPacket` and `GodModeState` are TypeScript types without zod schemas. Like `Campaign` itself, neither is parsed from untrusted input; both are produced by the engine, so a schema would guard nothing. `GodModeState` is documented in §7b.

## 9. Data provenance and presentation

### 9.1 Source attribution

All nation statistics are approximate, order-of-magnitude-correct public data from:
- **IMF World Economic Outlook** — GDP, growth, inflation, reserves
- **World Bank national accounts** — Population, demography, development indicators 
- **SIPRI Military Expenditure Database** — Defense budgets and personnel counts
- **CIA World Factbook** — Trade flows, energy, secondary confirmation

**Circa 2023**, hand-rounded to dataset units (USD millions for money fields). Treat every value as *plausible for game balance*, not as a cited figure for any use outside this game. Several economies (notably North Korea) do not publish reliable statistics and entries are informed estimates.

**Vendored seed for the full roster (2026-09-22).** `scripts/vendor-nation-stats.mjs` (network, run rarely; `pnpm vendor:nation-stats`) writes four pinned tables under `packages/world/src/dataset/seed/`, each with its source, license and fetch date in the header, and nothing downstream reads the network. Every figure cell is the latest non-null year in 2010–2024 and carries that year after an `@`; the early end of the window only fills a gap (Eritrea has published no national accounts since 2011).
- `nation-identity.tsv` — the roster of 196 governments (every CIA World Factbook archive entry listing UN membership, 193, plus Taiwan, Kosovo and Palestine), with codes, capital, continent, currency, languages, land neighbors and area from **GeoNames countryInfo** (CC BY 4.0) and short name, adjective, government type, capital coordinates, median age, coastline and whether the country keeps standing forces ("no regular military forces") from the **CIA World Factbook archive** (public domain).
- `nation-figures.tsv` — population, urbanization, GDP, growth, inflation, unemployment, reserves, exports, imports, birth, death and net-migration rates, literacy, military spending (SIPRI's, republished) and armed forces, energy import share, homicide rate (UNODC's, republished), aid received, the merchandise and service export breakdowns, and the five **Worldwide Governance Indicators**, all from the **World Bank API** (CC BY 4.0).
- `nation-fiscal.tsv` — general government gross debt (% of GDP), the agency-averaged sovereign rating index (21 = AAA, one notch per point, 1 = selective default), the share of debt in foreign currency, the effective interest rate (interest over the debt it was paid on) and the average maturity, from the **World Bank Fiscal Space database** (Kose, Kurlat, Ohnsorge and Sugawara 2022; CC BY 4.0 per the World Bank data catalog; refetched 2026-09-24). `node scripts/vendor-nation-stats.mjs --fiscal` generates `dataset/fiscal-anchors.ts` from it (`FISCAL_ANCHORS`, `FiscalAnchor`, each figure `{ value, year }`): what each government's books open on where the database has a figure (`sovereign.openingScore` holds the published notch inside the letter the nation carries, `openingRate`, `foreignRollover`). A government with no figure opens on the engine's labeled rule for its letter and its wealth.
- `nation-energy.tsv` — primary energy production and consumption, consumption by fuel, and coal, gas and oil production, in quadrillion Btu, from the **US EIA International Energy Statistics** bulk file (public domain).
- `macro-anchors.tsv` (`node scripts/vendor-nation-stats.mjs --anchors`, fetched 2026-09-23) — each government's growth, inflation and unemployment with the year and source of each figure, the latest non-null year in 2020–2024: **World Bank WDI** (CC BY 4.0; unemployment is the ILO modeled estimate) for 194 of 196 governments, and the United States from **BEA** NIPA A191RL real GDP growth and **BLS** CPI-U and U-3 annual averages for 2024 (2.8%, 2.95%, 4.02%; public domain). An empty cell has no anchor (Taiwan has none; North Korea, Yemen, Venezuela, Cuba, Syria and the DR Congo lack one or two rates). The generated module `dataset/macro-anchors.ts` (`MACRO_ANCHORS`, `MacroAnchor`, `MacroAnchorFigure`, `MACRO_ANCHOR_SOURCES`, `MACRO_ANCHORS_FETCHED`) is exported from the dataset barrel. The 60 hand-stated records' three rates were refreshed to their anchors at six decimal places, with Argentina's 219.9% held at the model's 200% ceiling. No zod schema changed; the engine rests each economy on its authored record (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, the coupling graph).

**The world seed tables (23 carried-conflicts rows, 49 basing rows, 5 munitions classes and three more seeded; `debt-rebase.tsv` still empty).** Seven tables under `packages/world/src/dataset/seed/`, each beside a TypeScript table in `dataset/` that holds the same rows and is empty until its owner seeds it with sourced rows (`seed-tables.test.ts` holds the two together and refuses a row without a source and an opened date): `carried-conflicts.tsv` (kind — interstate, civil, non-state, tension, deployment, sanctions — parties, since, status, mapped_state, mapping_rule, source, opened; `CARRIED_CONFLICTS`), `us-basing.tsv` (country, base as a neutral label, service, personnel_band, anchor, lat, lon, anchor_source, anchor_opened, source, opened; `US_BASING`: 49 rows, 35 sourced anchors, 5 zone-level installations and 9 country totals, coordinates from Wikidata items opened 2026-10-10 and listed in `sources/founding-data/forces-sources.md`), `us-munitions.tsv` (class, systems, opening_index, production_rate, lead_time, provenance — "sourced" or "planning value" — source, opened; `US_MUNITIONS`), `commodity-producers.tsv` (commodity, metric — production or exports — nation, share, source, opened; `COMMODITY_PRODUCERS`), `price-anchors.tsv` (commodity, series, period, value, unit, kind — historical, estimate, forecast, projection — source, opened; `PRICE_ANCHORS`), `price-shares.tsv` (reading, component, share, series, period, source, opened; `PRICE_SHARES`) and `debt-rebase.tsv` (nation, ratio, source, opened; `DEBT_REBASE`). `seed-tables.test.ts` holds an eighth the same way: `force-cost-anchors.tsv` (anchor, value, unit, dollar_year — the dollars a `usd_millions` row is stated in, empty on a count or an index — provenance — "sourced" or "planning value" — covers, source, opened; `FORCE_COST_ANCHORS`, 16 rows opened 2026-10-10 and listed with their quotes in `sources/force-costs-sources.md`): CPI-U for 2008, 2011, 2017, 2018 and 2026, the exercise, the carrier, air wing and destroyer's direct annual cost, the strike group's five escorts, Odyssey Dawn's cost and strike sorties, the brigade's shipping and its year in Iraq, and two planning values (two strike sorties an aircraft, seven days' passage). The engine prices every military rung from it (`force-costs.ts`). Two more follow the same contract, and `federal-sites.test.ts` holds them cell for cell: `federal-sites.tsv` (institution, site, state, location, lat, lon, hq_source, reason, source, opened; `FEDERAL_SITES`, 76 rows) and `state-capitols.tsv` (state, capitol, city, lat, lon, source, opened; `STATE_CAPITOLS`, 51 rows), both opened 2026-10-10. `dataset/debt-rebase.ts#applyDebtRebase` runs first in the `worldDataset` assembly (`dataset/index.ts`) and is the identity while its table is empty. `seed/nation-election-calendar.tsv` and its loader `dataset/election-calendar.ts` predate the contract. Israel's `military.arsenal.declared` is `false`; its record carries no warhead count. `commodity-producers.tsv` holds 79 rows (production and export shares from EIA-FAQ709, USDA-GRAIN/OILSEEDS-2026-09, USGS MCS 2025; lithium shares exclude the withheld US; the EU and the unverified secondary gas rows are left out), `price-anchors.tsv` 14 (EIA-STEO-2026-09, EIA-GASEXPLAINED/GASDIESEL, USDA-WASDE-675), `price-shares.tsv` 31 (EIA retail breakdown, USDA ERS food dollar with the two series kept apart, BLS CPI relative importance Dec 2025); `debt-rebase.tsv` is empty, so the rebase is still the identity. `dataset/election-calendar.ts` also dates each selection: `windowSelection(nation)` (the first date on or after the oath, or one term past a pre-oath date where the row names only that), `preOathSelection(nation)` (the pre-oath column, if it falls before 2029-01-20) and `termYears(nation)`. A month cell falls on the 15th (`MONTH_PRECISION_DAY`) and a year cell on June 15 (`YEAR_PRECISION_MONTH`). Both days are planning values, and each `DatedSelection` says so (`planningDay`) and gives its `standing`: `scheduled`, `estimated` or `conflict`.

The IMF World Economic Outlook is deliberately not a source: its data terms allow reuse without written permission only for noncommercial purposes.

**The bake (`scripts/bake-nations.mjs`, `pnpm bake:nations`, reads `packages/world/dist`, so build the world package first).** It writes `dataset/nation-figures.generated.ts` — `NATION_FIGURES`, one row of complete `NationStats` plus `NationFigureProvenance` for every roster government the authored roster does not already state in full (135 today) — and `JUDGED_READINGS`. A figure on a baked row is one of three kinds, and the provenance says which:
- **Sourced** — a published figure converted to the dataset's units (USD millions for money, fractions for rates, TWh for energy production and consumption). `vintage` is the year most of a row's figures describe; `older` lists the figures that describe an earlier year.
- **Prior** (`priors`) — a sourced field no source states for that country, filled from the median of the same ratio across its continent's sourced figures (openness, reserve months, debt share, military share, cost per soldier, energy per head and mix) or from a least-squares fit of sourced figures against each other (literacy on GDP per head; an unrated country's rating on GDP per head and government effectiveness). A country the World Bank lists no aid for received none. `trade.topExports` ranks the World Bank's export breakdown by dollar value (fuel follows domestic production only for a net energy exporter; a net importer's fuel exports are refined product, and coal is `mining`); where there is no usable breakdown, a net energy exporter lists the fuels EIA says it produces.
- **Judged** (`JUDGED_READINGS`) — the 0..1 readings the dataset authors by judgment (regime stability, legitimacy, press freedom, corruption, readiness, branch strength, lift, reach, and the rest) plus reserve headcount and aid given, fitted by least squares on the authored records' own readings against the governance indicators and the country's sourced mass, so a new country lands on the scale the shipped content was tuned against. The bake prints each fit's R². A country with no standing military holds its force readings at zero by the Factbook's word instead. Counter-intelligence is fitted on governance alone, not defense spending, and a country of fewer than a million people reads its continent's median homicide rate rather than its own (one killing in Tuvalu is ten per 100,000): fitted on military mass and raw small-state rates, the first cut read Tuvalu as a collapsing state. Warheads, delivery triads and bases abroad are zero: every nuclear state is authored.

A sourced figure outside the range `NationStats` models is held at that range's edge and listed in `clamped` (four Gulf and island death rates below 3 per 1,000, the Central African Republic's birth rate, Guyana's growth). The bake refuses to write when any baked row fails to parse as a `Nation` through `composeNation`, when composing any authored record with its baked figures changes it, or when the dataset as last built fails validation. It prints the drift between the authored records and their sources and changes none of them; the bake prints its drift report each run.

**The thin records (2026-09-22).** The 135 governments added to the roster are drafted once by `scripts/draft-nation-records.mjs` into six region files (`dataset/nations-{africa,americas,asia,europe,mena,oceania}.ts`, exported together as `ROSTER_DRAFTS`) and then belong to the content room. A thin draft states only what a person decides — the name as the game spells it, the charter, the region and capital coordinates, disaster exposure, temperament and posture — and no figure, agenda or red line. Each is drafted from a source or a stated prior (`scripts/lib/nation-roster-priors.mjs`): identity from the seed with spelling overrides (American usage, the seat of government a US embassy deals with, official languages); the charter from the Factbook's government type by the precedents the authored roster set; the region from the priors' table, which adds `region-central-america` and `region-caucasus` and renames Nigeria's region to `region-west-africa` (the engine keeps `region-sub-saharan-africa` in its command map for older saves); disaster exposure from a regional table with country overrides; the eight traits from a least-squares fit of the authored records' own traits against the governance indicators and sourced mass (R² 0.84–0.93 for legalism, reliability and transparency; 0.18 for opportunism and 0.36 for isolationism, which are weak priors); posture from the authored records' aggression cutoffs.

Every table that throws on a missing country covers them without a hand-written row per country:
- **Party systems** (`dataset/roster-tables.generated.ts`, baked by `bake-nations.mjs`): the Factbook's seated tickets in the chamber that makes governments, most seats first (up to three), with beliefs the mean of the ideologies Wikidata records each stating, placed on the game's axes by the priors' ideology table; five governing tickets whose Wikidata item states none the table maps (Serbia's SNS, Georgian Dream, El Salvador's New Ideas, Peru's Popular Force, Kenya's UDA) take their documented program from the priors' `STATED_IDEOLOGIES` instead of reading as their country's dead center. `contest` is `dominant-party` where the leading ticket holds 60% of seats in a country whose governance voice score is below −0.5, otherwise `multiparty`, with authored overrides where no party holds seats (monarchies that bar them, juntas, Pacific parliaments of independents: `none`; Eritrea `one-party`). A seated list named after a living politician is recorded as the party that leads it, because the dataset names no real living person.
- **Schools**: the country's universities still operating, from Wikidata, the two most widely documented (at least five Wikipedia editions) as its flagship and a regional school; a country with none of note (seven, among them Monaco) borrows its region's hub schools (the University of the South Pacific's Fiji campus, the University of the West Indies), which is where such a state's officials study. Nothing on `Education` flags this: the signal was authored (`abroad: boolean`) but never drawn as anything but `false`, and was deleted rather than left documented and dead.
- **Name banks** (`dataset/name-banks.vendored.ts`, 47 banks): the most frequent given names by sex and family names among men carried by people who hold the country's citizenship (Wikidata via qlever.dev, born since 1940, or of any birth year where too few were), baked by `scripts/bake-name-banks.mjs`. Traditions without family names (Burmese, Bhutanese, Mongolian) draw the second name from personal names. Five traditions whose people mostly carry no given-name or family-name statements (Bhutanese, Khmer, Lao, Mongolian, Nigerien) also read names off the same people's English labels, split by the order the tradition writes them in (`LABEL_ORDERS` in `scripts/vendor-name-banks.mjs`: Lao and Nigerien personal name first, Khmer family name first, Mongolian with the father's name in the genitive, Bhutanese all personal names), which is still Wikidata and still CC0. The mapping is `ROSTER_NAME_CULTURE` in `dataset/name-cultures.ts`; every country draws from its own tradition's bank. Rwanda moved from the Ethiopian bank to the Great Lakes bank it shares with Burundi. A nation with no culture mapped now throws rather than falling back to the Anglo bank.
- **Portrait heritage** (`dataset/portrait-heritage.ts`): a regional default with the countries whose population is not their neighbors' named (Trinidad, Guyana, Mauritius, Fiji, Madagascar).
- **Capitals** (`cities.ts`, baked by `bake-cities.mjs`, which now reads the built nations): Wikidata's latest population for the city the game names, a region-median share of the national population where no source states one (Antigua, Palau), never more than the country.
- **Government types** (`dataset/nation-government-types.generated.ts`, `GOVERNMENT_TYPES`): the Factbook's government type for all 196, the words a reader is told ("Communist party-led state", "Federal parliamentary democracy"). It is display text, read by `governmentType()` in player-core with the charter's name as the fallback; the charter stays the coarse model the simulation runs a government under, and six charters covering 195 countries is why China used to read "The Presidential Republic". Nine entries the archive leaves empty, stale or ungrammatical are overridden in the bake, each with its reason (Niger's junta, the rival governments of Libya and Yemen, Palestine, Afghanistan's emirate).
- **US goods trade** (`dataset/us-goods-trade.generated.ts`, `US_GOODS_TRADE`, from `seed/us-goods-trade.tsv` written by `scripts/vendor-us-trade.mjs`): what the United States imported from and exported to each roster government in goods in the latest complete year (2025), USD millions, from the Census Bureau's end-use tables (public domain). Some 140 five-digit end-use categories are mapped onto the 28 trade sectors in the vendor, three of them split by a stated prior (organic chemicals half pharmaceutical ingredients, electric apparatus 40% batteries and solar, other industrial machines 30% semiconductor equipment); what no sector covers (gold, furniture, toys, returned goods) is kept as `other`. Territories are folded into the government that holds them, as the map draws them (Hong Kong and Macau into China, Greenland into Denmark, Curaçao and Aruba into the Netherlands). A partner's rows sum to the Census country totals: Canada $381.9 billion imported and $333.6 billion exported in 2025.
- **Borders** (`dataset/nation-borders.generated.ts`): GeoNames' land borders between roster governments, united with the authored lists and made to run both ways by `applyGeography`, which also gives a coastal thin country its region's chokepoint dependence. The United States now borders Cuba (Guantanamo Bay), and the homeland cradle lists it.

**Composing a record (`dataset/compose-nation.ts`).** A record is a `NationDraft` — the authored layer (identity, region, temperament, doctrine) plus any figure it chooses to state — and `composeNation(draft, NATION_FIGURES[id])` lays it over the baked figures, the authored value winning wherever one is stated; `sectors` follows from `topExports` and the energy mix unless the draft states it. The 61 records written before the bake state every figure, so composing one returns it unchanged. A draft with no baked row and no figures of its own throws.

### 9.2 Realistic variance

To avoid artificial-looking precision, all figures include realistic variance:
- Population: ±2% variance from base estimates
- GDP and financials: ±3% variance to reflect year-to-year fluctuation 
- Military budgets: ±5% variance given estimation uncertainty for opaque economies
- Percentages (growth, inflation, unemployment): kept to realistic precision (1-2 decimal places)

**Why variance matters**: Perfectly round numbers (e.g., exactly 335,000,000 population) signal fiction. Real census data has irregular variance, and the game's dataset reflects that to maintain plausibility.

### 9.3 Presentation standards

The dossier page presents data with explicit context:

1. **Year and source attribution** — All economic and demographic figures indicate "2023" and "USD" where applicable
2. **Unit clarity** — GDP shown in billions USD, per capita in thousands USD, reserves in billions USD
3. **Data quality notes** — A footer reminds players these are estimates, not authoritative sources
4. **Zero-value explanation** — Nations with no standing military (e.g., Iceland) include context explaining why

This prevents the "video game numbers" impression while keeping the dataset game-usable.

### 9.4 Per-nation calibration rationale

Each nation entry includes a comment block explaining:
- Why the charter mapping was chosen (and where it's approximate)
- Why reserve/budget figures diverge from economic weight if applicable
- Which statistics are uncertain (opaque economies, collapsed states)

This is the dataset's audit trail — check comments above each `NationInput.parse()` call for details.

### 9.5 `Nation.partySystem`

How power is contested inside a country, and by whom.

```ts
partySystem?: {
 contest: "multiparty" | "dominant-party" | "one-party" | "none";
 parties: Party[]; // empty when contest is "none"
}
```

`Party` is the shape already used by `Charter.parties` — `id`, `name`,
`shortName`, `kind`, `beliefs` on the three `Person.ideology` axes, and
`cohesion`.

**Why it is on the nation and not the charter.** Twenty-one nations in the
dataset share `charter-presidential-republic` and twenty-one share
`charter-parliamentary-republic`. A slate hung on the charter gives China and
Brazil the same parties, so before this field no government abroad could carry
one at all — and every surface that wanted a party for a foreign national fell
through to the United States' own slate.

**`contest: "none"` is a finding, not a gap.** A monarchy fields no parties.
An absent `partySystem` means nobody researched the country; `contest: "none"`
with an empty array means somebody did, and there are none. Only the second may
be printed.

Authored in `packages/world/src/dataset/party-systems.ts` and attached by
`applyPartySystems` in the dataset pipeline. The leader table's governing
parties are held to the slates, which moved to meet it (2026-09-23): Hungary's
contest is `multiparty` with Tisza first; Kazakhstan's `party-kaz-amanat` is
replaced by `party-kaz-adilet`; `party-nld-d66`, `party-phl-pfp` and
`party-col-defensores` are added; and `GOVERNING_ADDITIONS` prepends the
governing party to the roster slates of Bulgaria, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Costa
Rica, Gabon, Guinea, Kiribati, Myanmar, Nepal, Somalia and Sao Tome and
Principe, which moves Gabon, Guinea, Kiribati, Myanmar and Somalia from
`contest: "none"` to `dominant-party`. The table's 136 junior coalition
partners are left off the slates on purpose (`OFF_SLATE` in the vendor, which
fails on any governing name it cannot place). The United States reads its slate
off its own charter, so there is one source for the tickets Congress caucuses on.

`Nation.partySystem` is **required** on an assembled nation and absent from
`NationInput`, which is what a country looks like before assembly runs. That
split is the honest one: the block is attached from its own authored table
rather than written on the country row, and `applyPartySystems` throws on a
nation it has no entry for rather than assembling a country with no politics.

**Validator** — `checkNationPartySystem` requires the block on every shipped
nation, requires at least one ticket wherever `contest` is not `"none"`,
refuses tickets on a nation that says it runs none, and refuses a party id
claimed by two countries.

**Read by** — `engine.people.generateWorldPool` (which ticket a national politician
carries), and any surface that prints the party word on a dossier.


## 10. Incident impacts and delayed instruments

`Campaign.impactEvents?` stores versioned `ImpactEvent` records. `ImpactSpec` is a runtime-validated discriminated union for strike, outbreak, cyber, industrial, natural and infrastructure mechanisms. Inputs declare exposure, units, evidence and calibration; accounting ownership is explicit for population and economics. State separates current compartments/disruption from cumulative harm and dated report ranges. `ImpactEventEffect` uses an expected revision; only new cumulative deaths affect live population. Complete fields, equations, units and limits: [IMPACT_MODEL.md](IMPACT_MODEL.md).

`IssuedAct` includes `IssuedInstrumentAct`: request, issue/due dates, status, reserved treasury, forecast, optional authored aftermath/context, and optional outcome/civilian casualty range. Preparing force packages are reserved in capacity reads. Old saves omit both new record kinds naturally.

Foreign-action support: `ResolutionRequest.support? = { mode: "unilateral" | "seek", partners: readonly NationId[] }`. Missing support means no solicited contribution, including old requests; it no longer grants automatic coalition bonuses. `ActionProjection.support?` contains the mode, responses (`nation`, `name`, `status`, `days`, `reason`) and `delayDays`. Draft responses are pending, not confirmations. `IssuedInstrumentAct.supportRequests?` retains those responses with `dueDate`; status is `pending | confirmed | refused | conditional`. Selected governments must be distinct from actor/target and present in the campaign. Economic/military support takes three simulated days; execution waits at least four days, leaving a cancellation window after answers. `advanceUntilAttention` returns existing `consequence-return` attention when those answers first arrive, before execution, so Continue cannot skip the opportunity to stand down. Answers use current trust, target ties and shared compacts; limited-strike partners may require additional civilian assessment. Only confirmed, still eligible contributions change odds/standing at execution. Refusal and unmet conditions give no bonus. Cancellation releases the existing escrow and force reservation. Old issued acts without support records continue their saved clock and receive no invented consent.

`ActionProjection.costBasis?: ForceCostBasis` (engine `force-costs.ts`) is present on every military rung: `{ rung, unitType?, units, perUnit, costIndex, reach, upFront, weekly }`, USD millions. `units` is the package's count, or the rung's preferred size when the request names no package (1 and no `unitType` for an exercise); `perUnit` the US up-front price of one; `costIndex` the actor's authored `military.budget / military.activeHeadcount` over the United States' (1 for the United States, and for a government with neither); `reach` the distance factor; `upFront = perUnit × units × costIndex × reach`, which is `-cost.treasury`; `weekly` the price of the units each week they stay committed, without `reach`. `ReplyProjection.costBasis?` carries the same object for an instrument reply. A force rung's standing commitment opens with `resource.amountPerTurn` equal to `weekly` (0 for a limited strike, whose sorties are priced all-in); `standingCommitment` takes either `shareOfGdp` or `amountPerTurn`. Nothing is saved: the basis is recomputed with every projection, so the save version is unchanged.

`ResolutionRequest.strikeObjective? = "military-capacity" | "logistics" | "infrastructure"` applies to limited strikes (military rung 4); omission preserves the existing military-capacity objective. `ActionProjection.strikeAssessment?` contains objective, label, targetContext, low/moderate confidence, basis, evidence gaps, serviceRisk and targetEffect. Mission success affects the actual shared resolver: military-capacity reduces readiness by the existing base 0.1 scaled/jittered amount; logistics uses 0.06 readiness plus 0.08 airlift/sealift capacity damage and a ten-day transport outage; infrastructure uses 0.03 readiness and a fourteen-day network outage. Both disruption missions use the existing incident service/output-loss consumer. Outcome amounts remain provisional. A failed objective does not apply these success effects. Civilian harm is an independent draw, and disruption-focused objectives carry provisional risk multipliers (logistics 1.15, infrastructure 1.35).

`civilianProtection? = "standard" | "additional-assessment"` is also limited-strike-specific. Additional assessment adds three preparation days and multiplies modeled civilian risk by 0.65; this is a provisional simulation assumption. `assessedSite? = { site: NonNullable<ImpactSpec["site"]>, exposure: ImpactExposure }` carries validated sourced evidence, not a player site selector. The ordinary planner calls `assessStrikeSite`: seed-stable fictional exposure contexts cover military support areas, logistics/transport, industrial districts, energy/communications, mixed residential areas, and rarer education/healthcare facilities. Profiles have weights 12/8/6/6/10/1/1 plus 12 weight for no site case; weights are transparent scenario assumptions, not country-specific observed probabilities. No urbanization statistic creates a site identity or child exposure. Primary mission context is independently stable within the selected objective (installation/air defense/depot, supply/transport/logistics, or energy/transport/industrial/communications).

`SecurityState.strikeSiteAssessments?` stores `CivilianSiteAssessment` records keyed by `strike-assessment:<actor>:<target>:<turn>:<objective>`, retaining actor/target, issuedDate, `modeled-intelligence` source, mission reading, site, exposure and evidence gaps. `AssessmentEffect.siteIssue?` persists the record at authorization. Preview does not mutate state or draw RNG; the issued objective/evidence survive reload and delayed execution. Classification begins unconfirmed and does not assert victims' identities or ages. Delayed primary impacts retain `parentId = IssuedInstrumentAct.id`; collateral components of a successful disruption mission retain the mission incident as parent and `accounting.economy = parent`, preventing duplicate economic pressure. Physical deaths remain self-owned and independent. The execution ledger `causedBy` joins all components. Report revisions only charge new cumulative losses; the issued report's chance is updated from its execution projection.



`TradeMeasure` may carry `expiresDate` and `direction: imports | both`. A measure with both is a timed import suspension and lifts as one; a standing import embargo (an inherited one) lifts as an embargo and thaws the relationship. `coercionsInForce` reads whether a measure withholds the imposer's own sales the way the trade book (`trade-volume.ts`) reads the same measure: `stopsImposerSales` is true for an `export-control` and for an embargo that is not `direction: "imports"`, and false for `sanctions`, `asset-restriction` and an import-only embargo, so for those the growth bite, the partner cut-off and the commodity sanction shock read only the target's side. Every inherited OFAC program is a `sanctions` measure or an import-only embargo, so none of them takes US output off a commodity market: their export prohibitions are narrow (EO 14068's luxury goods and banknotes for Russia), farm and medical trade is licensed, and the recorded opening already carries what they left of trade with the target. A standing embargo another government declared (`embargo:<imposer>:<target>`) still withholds that government's sales. Position commitments may declare `importSuspensionDays`; the duration starts at signature. `ScenarioImpact.financial.scope` distinguishes a city week from a national one (a city week is priced one severity down; DECISIONS §6.16), and `human.accounting: already-reported` prevents a previously reported toll from being subtracted again. `ScenarioImpact.financial.basis` (`{ quantity, unit, unitCostUSD, weeks?, source }`, quantity and unit cost positive, weeks an integer from 1 to 520, source at least 12 characters) states the reply's own arithmetic in 2026 US dollars; with it the money is quantity × unit cost × weeks rather than the band's anchor. Impact pricing model version is 5. City pricing requires a valid positive local population. `resolveImpact` takes an optional sixth argument, `CostPricing` (`{ severity?, key? }`): the week's severity (absent prices at serious) and the draw key, a campaign seed with the occurrence it prices (absent prices at the center, unjittered). `SCENARIO_COST_ANCHORS` (world dataset, the `ForceCostAnchorRow` shape) carries the sourced and planning anchors.


### Issued intelligence and report sharing (2026-10-12)

`CollectionProduct` retains its identity, tasking, agency, discipline, target,
question, day/date, evidence weight and summary. New products also carry a
typed target kind, `findings | no-result` status, confidence, itemized findings
(topic, observation, source and observation day), explicit gaps and exposure.
These additions are optional only for legacy products. Unknown legacy evidence
is not reconstructed from today's world. `IntelligenceState.archive` preserves
issued products; `products` selects current support, which expires after the
collection cycle or when the tasking closes. Both are written by the existing
`intelligence` effect. An issued product is a snapshot, never a live selector.

`CollectionFinding.basis` may snapshot a `border-incident` with its persisted
incident ID, ordered pair of governments, assessment turn, `likely-inadvertent |
undetermined` finding and `low | moderate` evidence confidence. This variant is
written only after that incident has a recorded assessment, and only while the
assessment is within the collection cycle. It belongs to the `external-intent`
topic for a party to the incident. An unresolved incident creates no finding;
neither outcome names an initiator. The finding's observation and source quote
the assessed conclusion and available records. A later incident revision does
not change a product or recipient snapshot already issued.

`CollectionShare` stores report ID, recipient `NationId`, day/date and the issued
report snapshot. Optional `disclosedFindingIndexes` identifies positions in the original immutable report; its absence on a legacy receipt means the whole report was shared. `shareCollectionReport` validates recipients, refuses foreign dissemination of domestic surveillance, and appends only newly disclosed findings. Exact retries have no effects. Recipient snapshots contain only selected findings, a neutral summary and no withheld gaps; older receipts remain unchanged. No report unlocks all hidden target data. Sharing has no
automatic diplomatic bonus. A fresh moderate/high-confidence issued covert-pursuit finding can support the named recipient's requested economic participation against that report's target. Existing compact and competing target-tie checks apply, and military requests receive no such exception. Findings retain a typed pursuit snapshot; later live-world changes cannot rewrite the warning.
Save v65 reads v51–v65 (v65 adds the optional `ScenarioFamilyRun.place`, `ScenarioFamilyRun.unanswered` and `Campaign.flagPlaces`, all absent on an older save, whose arcs then draw their next scene's place as before) and preserves legacy notes without inventing findings or
past recipients. The Security report archive and sharing actions read these
same records through player-core.

Collection selection is bounded to one, two or four findings according to existing evidence weight (two for direct surveillance, four for public state analysis). The eligible pool includes actual pursuits, force/support commitments and current conditions plus the existing capability estimates. Discipline filtering happens before selection. A seeded topic-interleaved sequence advances across collection cycles, so later eligible facts are reachable without recomputing old reports. Public state analysis applies the same rotation to recorded conditions and governor requests. Success of collection and exposure are separate; a failed or exposed mission can still yield useful reporting, while a no-result report does not certify safety. Fresh report selection consumes no mutable RNG state when a dossier is read.

## 11. Person behavior: derived readings and fields

- `OrderProjection.review?` — the Attorney General's review (`certify-order` willingness, objects or clears) when the order's reach is 0.15 or more; `OrderProjection.execution?` — the executing chair's `carry-out` reading with the weeks a rule's effect is pushed. `signOrder`'s packet carries `counsel` (when the AG objects) and `provenance.decision`.
- `ActionTiming.pace?` (`ActionPace`) — the executing chair's added or saved days, already inside `days`; the issued operation's packet carries `provenance.decision`.
- `PendingRule.effectiveTurn` may run up to six weeks past `RULEMAKING_TURNS` when the chair drags.
- A directive's issuing packet carries the `carry-out` stamp; `Directive.status` `slow-walked` can now come from the chair as well as the institution.
- `CabinetTie.record` (`{ agreed, opposed, term }`) and `CabinetTie.terms` (`TieTerms`: ideology, temperament, turf, ambition, faction, record) — derived on read, never stored.
- `ScandalOrigin` `cabinet-leak` gains `target?`; an aimed leak's scandal implicates the target and their chair.
- Engagement and treaty acceptance stamps may carry `envoy` and `rapport`; `signTreaty` now stamps acceptance on both success packets.

### Legislative agenda catalogue extension (2026-10-10)

`LegislativeAct` has optional player-facing `topic` and `tradeoff` strings. These
are code-owned catalogue metadata, not saved bill fields. Eighteen domestic
packages in `legislative-acts-domestic.ts` join the existing `LEGISLATIVE_ACTS`
registry. Their `arises(Campaign)` predicates remain engine-only; presentation
receives eligible proposals and never the predicates or their thresholds.

No Campaign, Bill, save-version, nation or demographic shape changes. Existing
saves discover the catalogue when loaded. Enacted bill IDs continue to select
annual `LAW_OFFSETS`, their spans, and any macro resting offsets. New program
funding is charged there only, with no second enactment debit to presidential
spending authority. New fiscal values are explicit simulation assumptions for
fictional legislation, not estimates of real bills. Topic labels do not create
new statistics: the engine still has no health-coverage, racial composition,
discrimination-case or housing-unit count.


### Occurrence and remedy compatibility (2026-10-12)

`CastScenario.occurrence?` freezes later repeatable non-family occurrences as `templateId@turn`. The first occurrence and legacy, once-only and family casts retain their old identity. Case, docket, attention, disposition, URL and silence-charge identities use the occurrence key; case subject and content pin retain the authored template ID. Completed case history supplies the existing class cooldown for repeatable templates. Legacy snapshots keep their identity and known history.

`ExecutiveOrderRecord` and `PendingRule` optionally retain `nationContribution`, the realized post-clamp non-debt path movements at landing. The `orderRemedy` effect debits the known contribution once and records `remedyApplied`, `remedyContribution` and, for missing legacy measurements, `remedyUnquantified`. Effective rules can become `struck-down`. No debt or sunk spending is refunded. For new attributable relative movements, `Campaign.orderStatLineage` also preserves later saturation; absolute writes or unexplained drift invalidate that path and leave relief explicitly unquantified.

Domestic state collection is `targetKind: domestic-state`, DHS I&A, OSINT and the fixed public-record question. It reads modeled public conditions and governor requests, identifies its public source and gaps, and has no covert-exposure draw. Neither state unrest nor presidential direction authorizes resident surveillance; both locale entry points refuse that act without charging or creating a report.

`CollectionTasking.focus?` is a typed collection scope: `overview`, `external-intent`, `force-posture`, `economic-pressure`, or `domestic-public-conditions`. The first four apply to a foreign government; the last is DHS I&A's public state analysis. `collectionTopics` intersects the focus with the chosen discipline's actual finding topics before any cycle selection. An incompatible pair is unavailable before authorization, while unlawful domestic requests still create the existing counsel refusal. `question` holds the canonical focus question on new taskings and remains the original text on old saves. Optional `note` is trimmed and recorded on taskings and products but never parsed as an instruction or used to choose findings. Older taskings without `focus` retain general reporting within their discipline and their original question; their new reports name the limitation in `gaps`. Issued `CollectionProduct` archives and disclosure snapshots retain their own focus, note, findings, dates, confidence and gaps without retroactive reinterpretation.

**Person-level collection is bound to a case.** `CollectionTasking.subject?`, `CollectionProduct.subject?` and `BoundaryRefusal.subject?` hold an `IntelSubject` `{ personId, snapshotDay, basis }` (`intel-authority.ts`), where `basis` is `{ kind: "pursuit", caseId, actor, openedTurn }` (an active foreign pursuit of the United States; the person is that government's seated leader) or `{ kind: "inspector-general-matter", caseId, institution, causeId }` (a public inspector-general matter whose cause is a recorded graft event; the person is `causal.graft.person`). The engine derives these leads from existing state in `selectSubjectLeads`; no person, case or residence is stored or invented. `TaskingTargetKind` `foreign-person` and `domestic-person` require a subject. `CollectionTasking.closedReason?` is written when the engine closes a tasking whose case no longer holds. `CollectionProduct.targetKind` also admits `foreign-person` (target is the person id, `subject` is copied from the tasking and therefore into every `CollectionShare.report` snapshot). `IntelligenceState.agReferrals?` is an append-only list of `AgReferral` `{ id, subject, day, authority }`: a presidential referral of a domestic matter to the Attorney General, which directs nothing and opens no investigation. `INTEL_AUTHORITY` names the only two legal sources the desk cites, each keyed to a file in `docs/player-rebuild/authority-sources/` and checked against its text by `packages/content/src/intel-authority-sources.test.ts`. All of these fields are optional and no schema version changed: an older save reads them as absent. On every load, `normalizeCampaign` runs `withSubjectTaskingsRevalidated`, which closes (with `closedReason`) any open person-level tasking that has no subject or whose person or case no longer holds; a tasking opened by an older build against a free-text "person" is closed this way.


### Individual congressional seats and recorded votes (2026-10-12)

`Congress.members?` stores `CongressMember {seat, chamber, state, person, party, seatClass?}`. `state` is the represented constituency, not a birthplace inference. The roster must contain one distinct person per voting seat, 435 House districts and 100 Senate state/class seats. Current leadership and committee holders must fit an eligible voting seat; an unmatched post remains vacant rather than inventing a holder. Existing generated US role people supply identities; a bounded reserve is generated at the seal for electoral replacements, outside the public founding shortlist. A supported old save without members receives prospective deterministic membership; it receives no invented historical individual votes.

`ChamberVote` now optionally retains `rollCall` rows with person, seat, state, party, yea, support and reasons; `procedure` distinguishes cloture and final passage, and `cloture?` retains the Senate's preceding question. `recordedTurn?` is required before the UI may treat an individual call as recorded: `Bill.passage` can hold an introduction-time projection and is not itself proof that a vote happened. A failed cloture has a recorded procedural vote but no recorded final vote. Historical aggregate-only votes keep their totals and no individual votes are reconstructed. The stored support score and internal reasons are simulation data; the ordinary member list and roll-call view display identities, constituencies and completed yea/nay facts only.

### Judicial service and ordinary vacancies (2026-10-12)

Supreme Court `InstitutionRuntime` seats optionally retain `judicialProvenance {serviceStartYear, appointingParty, synthetic?: true}` and `judicialAssessmentYear`. Founding service and appointing party are synthetic campaign history, not observed historical appointments. Confirmation records the actual simulated service start and administration party. `PersonRuntime.judicialRetired?: true` excludes a retired justice from the judicial shortlist while preserving their identity and historical opinions. Death uses the existing alive/current-role and institution-vacancy effect paths. Missing legacy service provenance is seeded and stored at a prospective assessment; past votes remain untouched.

### Report-bound authored occurrences (2026-10-12)

`ScenarioCondition.kind = "collectionReport"` selects issued findings by `targetKind` (foreign-nation/domestic-state), typed finding `topic`, optional minimum `confidence`, `exposed` and `shared` state. `ScenarioCast.pick = "collectionReport"` binds `target`, `recipient` or `home`. A recipient requires a recorded share; home requires a public state report. `CastScenario.report?` freezes the issued source and selected share, and is serialized into the presidential case snapshot. Its occurrence key includes report and, for recipient follow-up, share identity, rather than selecting new facts when reopened. Expired/withdrawn source evidence closes the case without an inaction charge. The status gate distinguishes findings from no-result; an empty product must have zero findings and explicit gaps. A close-tasking reply requires `activeTasking:true`: matching open task identity, target, agency, discipline and question at casting and revalidation. A direct surveillance report without a tasking cannot satisfy that gate.

Report interpolation uses explicit tokens for report identity, summary, selected observation/source, confidence, exposure, target, recipient, age and finding count. Tokens read the frozen source, never hidden live pursuit truth. Classified foreign report answers use intelligence feed visibility and cannot author a public press line. Domestic public-state findings remain public evidence and do not authorize private resident investigation.


### Scoped executive authority and construction funds (2026-10-12)

`Campaign.externalEmergencies?` stores `ExternalThreatEmergency` records with target nation, observed pursuit ID, statutory finding, declared/expiry turns and optional end/provenance. A recent active overt pursuit against the United States supplies the observed threat; the finding must match its aim. A state disaster declaration cannot substitute. `DeclaredEmergency.cause` also permits `armed-conflict`, with `armedForcesConflictId` identifying an actual war involving the United States.

`OrderSubject` binds a selected foreign nation, state/condition and recovery authority, military construction project and declaration, or enacted policing-grant bill. Grant conditions must stay within that law's reporting, investigation and nondiscrimination terms. Relief requires the selected state's actual eligible disaster request, separate Stafford declaration and fixed recovery appropriation. The construction route requires the armed-forces declaration and the selected project's unobligated balance. The signature records its exact subject and redirected amount, cancels that project's remaining schedule atomically, and never pays those already-appropriated dollars a second time from Treasury.

`Campaign.militaryConstructionProjects?` contains named `MilitaryConstructionProject` records: stable ID, name, optional host state, appropriation bill, appropriated/obligated/redirected USD millions, enactment turn and active/canceled status. Source authority, obligations and cash are separate: optional `sourceSpent` stores paid source commitments, `lastCashOutlay` stores the last processed week’s combined source/redirected cash (USD millions), and `lastWorkTurn` guards repeated processing. Redirected authority, `redirectedObligated`, and `redirectedSpent` are separate. `redirectedLastTurn` retains the legacy redirected-work guard; `redirectedFirstContractDay` and `redirectedContractId` record the actual first contract. `ExecutiveOrderRecord.constructionNotice?` stores congressional receipt day, permitted contract day, destination state/project, direct military support, canceled-source impact, estimated cost and contract ID. The domestic route caps aggregate transfers under a declaration at $100 million and waits five calendar days before contracting. Interim or final adverse relief stops future commitments while existing obligations continue paying. Enacted law creates source funds; GDP changes do not rewrite the original dollar appropriation. Old saves receive no invented projects, notices or contracts. Current law outlays annualize the recorded weekly cash, with zero additional generic investment smoothing because that cash already counts as purchases. The ten-year reader instead divides remaining payable source/redirected obligations and still-authorized future commitments over the horizon. Canceled uncommitted source balances are excluded; actual liabilities can pay after the original obligation year. Missing legacy cash remains unrecorded rather than invented.

### Dated member outreach (2026-10-12)

`IssuedMemberLobbyAct` extends the existing issued-act clock with member, live bill ID, optional broker and coalition scope. Its forecast remains unknown until the response arrives. `LobbyRecord` may identify a member/bill and directed `PersonCooperation` events, each carrying source, target, act ID, date, acceptance and account. Biography overlap is not a cooperation record.

`PersonRuntime.issuePressure?` holds bounded dated pressure with act ID, source, delta, expiry and account. Only a vote on the matching act may read it; expiry, ineligible participants and repeated resolution cannot create extra pressure. Save/load preserves the same issued request and recorded response. Public readers use known facts and dated cooperation; they do not expose private future roll-call scores.


### Attributable remedies, legal participation and live casualty accounting (2026-10-12)

`Campaign.orderStatLineage?` maps each affected non-debt policy path to an `OrderStatLineage`: its initial base, ordered intended relative movements, optional order/rule owner IDs, and current owner weights. `NationStatEffect.requestedDelta?` records the path and intended amount before clamping; `skipLineage` is reserved for the replay writer. Consecutive unowned movements compact into an equivalent clamped translation with lower/upper bounds. History is not dropped at an arbitrary event count. Limited relief adjusts one owner's weight; final removal sets it to zero. Replaying the full sequence preserves unrelated interventions even at saturation. Absolute sets, missing legacy attribution or live drift do not permit a fabricated counterfactual. `CourtProceeding.partyRelief.unquantified?` and order/rule remedy markers retain that limitation for the case reader. Debt and spent funds are excluded.

`ExecutiveOrderRecord.legalReviewer?` retains the actual advising person and review day at signing. If that person later participates as a justice in the same matter, eligibility excludes them for prior government participation. `CourtMatter` retains parties, injury, jurisdiction, legal question and authority, disputed facts, sought relief and affected interests; optional arguments/evidence and state apply to the supported statutory, agency-procedure and federal/state families. `PendingRule.process?` records notice weeks, answered comments and preserved site review at the time of agency action. Procedural litigation reads these facts after the rule becomes final, rather than inventing a completed violation at presidential signature.

`populationToll` is a relative physical casualty effect with nation, deaths and an optional state place. It debits the live national population and corresponding state populations at application, including a delayed authored answer. It cannot write a stale cast-time absolute population over subsequent births/deaths. The existing allocator handles unlocated losses and overflow; foreign losses without modeled subdivisions remain national. Political reactions remain separate channels.

### Reconciliation and authored legislative transmission (2026-10-12)

`Congress.budgetResolutions?` stores the fiscal year, budget subject, instructed committee, exact act and bill occurrence, requested turn, recorded chamber votes and adopted status. A concurrent resolution passes both chambers without presidential signature and grants no enacted benefit. Only an adopted instruction in the current fiscal year for that bill occurrence permits its eligible budget-only catalogue act to use reconciliation. Legacy saves without such a record use ordinary procedure. Reusing the act ID in a later bill does not reuse the instruction.

A position `ReplyCommitment.transmit?` names an actual administration legislative act. The scenario `legislationAvailable` condition and audit validate the same act and sponsor; projection and commitment call the existing `transmit` resolver. Its normal availability, attention cost and bill creation apply; no spending or enacted benefit lands at transmission. Typed actions cannot combine an arbitrary authored charge with the resolver's charge.


`ChamberVote.castingVote? {person, yea}` separately identifies the Vice President's additional ballot on an eligible tied simple-majority question. New `yeas`/`nays` include that ballot, while the member roll call and caucus counts still contain only actual senators. Old recorded votes remain unchanged and retain their older `tieBrokenBy` attribution. Cloture and supermajority questions cannot gain a casting ballot.


### Recorded cooperation, bound commands and election access (2026-10-12)

`CausalProvenance.internalDecisions?` retains private recipient `DecisionStamp` responses for engine replay and audit. It is optional in old saves and is not player evidence. Normal Record and dossier readers must omit it; neither a classified feed label nor a public break authorizes revealing recipients' private scores. Cabinet opposition considers current office or recorded counsel access and distinct living outgoing contacts. A previous accepted issue discussion permits an approach, not automatic support for a future leadership challenge.

`ReplyCommitment.tariff?` binds the response to an actual active retaliation measure by ID, actor, sector, rate and imposed turn. `CastScenario.tradeMeasure?` freezes those facts into the occurrence. The `tradeMeasure` condition also declares the response sector/rate and checks the existing tariff resolver. `ReplyCommitment.directive?` binds an actual open inspector-general matter by ID, institution, subject and opening turn. Projection and commitment revalidate their source before calling the existing tariff/directive resolver; an expired source cannot be substituted with a newer unrelated matter.

The independent election-access producer adds optional `StateRuntime.electionAccess` with election/observation turns, audit actor, compliant polling-place share, `responseDueTurn`, recorded `response`, seeded `installationCapacity`, and prospective `remedyDueTurn`/`remediedShare` fields; `StateEffect.setElectionAccess` writes it through the existing state effect owner. `CourtSubject` includes `state-election-access`, `CourtMatter.kind` includes `election-access`, and a completed `CourtCase` no longer requires an order when its typed subject is independent. `StateCall.access` describes the affected disability cohort's participation; `ElectionResult.stateCalls` freezes actual election-night calls so a later remedy cannot rewrite a certified result. Absence in a supported old save records no earlier audit or call; no historical finding is invented. The audit precedes the state response and Attorney General filing by two turns. Interim installation remains in force through the lower judgment; denial of a stay is distinguished from a merits appeal. Recorded capacity may leave an access gap at Election Day.


`ConversationScript.locales?` carries where a scenario decision happens as causal-stamp locale ids (state then city at home, the city alone abroad), set by `castBound` from the resolved locus; `decisionProvenance` copies it onto the answer's `CausalProvenance.locales`, `stampedDecision` unions it with any locale the resolver stamped, and `inactionPacket` stamps the same ids on a week left unanswered. `eventPlace` (player-core) pins a Record entry at that city when it lies in the stamped state or government. `ConversationScript.visibility?` carries the visibility of a frozen decision. Report decisions use intelligence provenance; private inspector-general decisions use internal provenance and produce no public feed item. The presidential Record still reads its own private decision through the foreground ledger. Typed inspector-general commitments bind the originating open matter and select investigation, general enforcement posture with an explicit subject, or an inspector-removal notice with a reason. Each calls the existing resolver; no public-disclosure toggle is inferred.


`ActionProjection.successExposureChance?` is the conditional probability of public attribution after covert mission success. It is separate from success odds and failure branches. Covert resolution records the resulting visibility in existing packet provenance; no new historical exposure is invented for old records.


### Dated service complaints and intercepted evidence (2026-10-12)

`ServiceRuntime.entryRefusals?` keeps a family-visa refusal's ID, exact entry-suspension order record, decision turn and ground. An actual decision in the consular queue precedes the immigration proceeding. Removing a proclamation does not grant a visa or invent admission.

`ServiceRuntime.housingComplaint?` stores a dated HUD complaint, respondent, original/current application deadline, alleged racial denial, and the provider's recorded `race | neutral-capacity` criterion. Allegation and finding are separate. Its status tracks pending, interim protection, ordered reconsideration, completed reconsideration or ended protection. A private §3613 action reaches only that applicant/provider; reconsideration does not guarantee a tenancy.

`ServiceRuntime.wageComplaint?` stores one investigated timecard/payroll, hours, regular hourly rate, paid/required wages (dollars), substantiated good-faith evidence, dated employer response and recovery state. The service produces the record before litigation; refusal of supervised payment can produce the Secretary's worker-specific §216(c) suit. Wages and liquidated damages remain a private employer liability, not federal outlays.

`IntelligenceState.surveillanceIncident?` stores an actual agency interception before Inspector General discovery: stable identity, agency, target/location, acquired content, intent, consent/order/exception facts, intercept turn/day, audit log, exposure turn and liability/compliance status. `damages` is dollars. A refused presidential tasking cannot produce this record. The IG exposes existing evidence; the resulting §1810 matter concerns the aggrieved person and responsible officials. Subsequent agency deletion is a compliance act, not a remedy attributed to §1810. The optional records survive saves; old saves receive no invented prior complaint or interception.

The existing `CourtSubject`/`CourtMatter` unions include `fair-housing-enforcement`, `wage-hour-enforcement` and `unlawful-surveillance`; their typed IDs point back to the dated source. The court record retains its frozen parties, evidence, procedure and final opinion. Lower-court cases contain no invented Supreme Court votes.

### Live-bill authored decisions (2026-10-12)

`ScenarioCast.pick = "liveBill"` requires exactly one `ScenarioCondition.kind = "liveBill"` gate with `stage: markup | conference` and, for conference, `responseText: base | targeted`. It binds a real in-progress amendable bill whose corresponding engine action is available. `CastScenario.bill?` freezes its ID, act, title, introduction turn, stage and chamber/text state. Optional snapshots preserve legacy cases without backfilling current facts.

`{{billTitle}}` and `{{billProvision}}` read that frozen subject. A position commitment's typed `billText` action proposes the targeted provision or recommends the selected conference text through the existing legislative resolver. Occurrence identity includes the authored template and bill occurrence; the frozen snapshot retains its stage. Reopening never silently changes the bill; stage, status or text changes withdraw the obsolete decision without charging silence, and commitment revalidates the exact source. Authored prose cannot declare passage or enactment from a recommendation.


### Enacted-law succession (2026-10-12)

`Bill.revises?` persists `{billId, kind: repeal | renewal | replacement, provision?: base | targeted}` at introduction. The predecessor is an exact enacted bill occurrence. Renewal snapshots its enrolled provision; replacement selects the authored targeted housing provision. The three housing revision measures pass through the ordinary legislative stages and presidential disposition. Repeated original housing enactments in legacy saves fold as successive renewals: the latest is operative while every enactment stays historical. `hasLawRevisionTakenEffect` lets the document distinguish a stale enactment without effect.

`selectLaws` retains every historical enactment. `selectCurrentLaws` folds enacted succession in stable enactment order, removing only an operative predecessor; a stale competing revision cannot revive a superseded program. Introduction, failure and veto cannot alter the predecessor. Fiscal sunset ends the specified flow without deleting the operative statute. Enacted repeal stops continuing flows, renewal starts a new authorized horizon without replaying prior one-off effects, and replacement changes future substance. Previously spent cash, borrowed debt and historical effects remain. Outstanding construction liabilities are priced from the historical source bill even when new commitments are no longer authorized.


### Stafford declarations and fixed recovery authority (2026-10-12)

`StateRuntime.staffordRequest?` records the governor's request ID, exact condition ID/cause, request turn, beyond-capacity finding, response-plan status, resources committed and cost-share acceptance. `staffordDeclaration?` records declaration ID, matching request ID and declaration turn. `declareStaffordDisaster` uses these predicates through the existing state effect. A National Emergencies Act declaration cannot substitute for this major-disaster declaration.

Every route by which `act-disaster-recovery` becomes law stamps `Bill.recoveryAuthority? {appropriated, throughTurn}` from enactment GDP and the authored funding horizon. `appropriated` is USD millions, not a later GDP share. Legacy laws without this record have unknown nominal authority and cannot fund a new allocation. A relief `OrderSubject` names state, cause, declaration ID and bill ID; the signed `ExecutiveOrderRecord.subject` also retains the allocation `amount` in USD millions. Allocation consumes the fixed unallocated balance and is unavailable after its horizon. Signature does not make a second Treasury or debt charge. Repeated authorization uses the existing order identity/idempotency gate.

Report-driven relief binds the same available subject at casting. When a report has no matching declaration and unallocated appropriation, a typed `reliefRefusal` retains a visible disabled answer rather than dropping its command and applying only the prose. Both projection and commitment enforce it; a frozen eligible subject is rechecked when committed.


### Order rescission and statutory expiry (2026-10-12)

`ExecutiveOrderRecord.rescindedTurn?`, `endReason?: "rescinded" | "sunset"`, and `sunsetDay?` retain the end of an order separately from its signature and judicial record. `sunsetDay` is an absolute campaign day, stamped ninety days after signature for the temporary price-control authority. Old records without a recorded sunset do not invent a historical deadline. `PendingRuleStatus` includes `rescinded` for a rule withdrawn after taking effect; a pending or stayed rule becomes `canceled`.

The `orderRescission` effect records that end idempotently. `rescindOrder(campaign, recordId)` resolves an exact signed occurrence, refuses one already ended or struck down, and emits the existing `orderRemedy` contribution-lineage operation when applicable. `orderExpiryPhase` uses the same operation on its recorded day. The signed order and court proceeding remain in history. Prospective attributable stat effects end; spent cash, debt, prior payments and unrelated interventions remain. Construction authority checks the ended record before admitting a new obligation. Legacy attribution remains unknown where no lineage exists.


### Opportunity-dependent cabinet graft (2026-10-12)

`CausalProvenance.graft?` stores the responsible person, role, institution, fiscal year, budget account, annual GDP share, department ownership share, annual purchasing exposure, normalized purchasing reach and oversight pressure observed at the act. `annualPurchasing` is USD millions of exposed purchasing authority, **not cash diverted**; no contract-level diversion amount is modeled. Shares/reach/pressure are bounded fractions. The optional snapshot lives on the existing action ledger and survives a successor or save/reload. Absence means no recorded graft act, never proof of clean conduct.

The live non-transfer budget allocation and existing account-to-department ownership produce the opportunity. A shutdown or absent spending line supplies none. One successful steering act per department role/fiscal year is recorded; replay or replacing its holder cannot reuse that opportunity. Principle, ambition, reach and oversight enter the reserved `steer-contracts` willingness question. The one effect is a bounded institution-integrity loss, presently 0.02–0.06 depending on purchasing reach; that provisional coefficient is not calibrated by this change. Its current downstream consumer is audit warrant, not an invented diverted-dollar charge or a second generic implementation multiplier.

`OversightMatter.causeId?` links delayed inspector discovery to the original causal event. Seated inspectors and current pressure govern when that record is reached. Opening and evidence-gathering text reports suspected purchasing irregularities without disclosing the person or hidden cause. At the reported transition, the finding names its original responsible person and links the recorded act, even if a successor now holds office. `graftDisclosed` permits normal player history only after a public inspector finding beyond the opened stage; Record rows/details/causal counts, person named-in history and office records apply it. Disclosed dossier copies retain the funding snapshot and original actor but omit private willingness terms. Ordinary internal presidential acts retain their existing visibility. This is a specific concealed-misconduct boundary, not a reinterpretation of all internal history.

### Bound institutional decisions (2026-10-12)

`FrozenJudicialVacancy` identifies the institution, office, seat and `vacancyOpenedDay`, plus two distinct presently eligible nominee IDs, names and public career/education descriptions. The instance day is computed from the campaign day and institution's vacant-day count, so a later vacancy in the same seat gets a new occurrence. Both authored nomination alternatives call `openNomination`; neither confirms or seats a justice. Runtime revalidation refuses a filled/replaced vacancy, changed identity or ineligible nominee.

`FrozenBill` retains the actual bill ID, act, title, introduction turn, markup/conference stage, a copied chamber-text state and optional `conferenceDeadline`. The engine derives the conference deadline from its actual sitting schedule; the opportunity clock and staged docket close no later than that date. New casts at or after the deadline are unavailable, and a late cast receives only the remaining time. A `liveBill` gate may limit its act and request `responseText: "both"`; this requires two differing texts that actually passed the House and Senate and two valid recommendations. The authored choice freezes and renders each passed provision, then invokes `recommendBillConferenceText`. A recommendation never enacts the bill or guarantees the conference vote. Stale bill stage/text invalidates the pending choice rather than charging inaction against a different bill.

`OrderSubject` adds `rule-review` with a target `ruleId` and `lease-planning` with a sourced `areaId`. A rule-review finding stores `targetRuleId` and `retain | recommend-revision | closed-without-action` on `PendingRule.reviewFinding`; the rule's resolution turn dates it. Review does not repeal the target. The planning subject identifies a BOEM program area and remains subject to the program's end date. Planning confers no lease, permit or production. Subsequent transaction delivery remains a separate implementation requirement.


### Offshore sale and receipt records (2026-10-12)

`Campaign.offshoreLeaseSales?` is an optional array of `OffshoreLeaseSale` records, written by the `offshoreLeaseSale` effect. Each names a sourced program area, the exact operative planning-rule occurrence and a clearly modeled tract. It stores environmental/consultation/notice dates, seeded qualified bids, a modeled fair-value floor, an award or no-sale decision, and a separate exploration-plan review. No record means no modeled sale; legacy saves do not acquire a historical lease. Amounts are USD millions; work clocks use campaign turns. The review obligation and Interior capacity are frozen at initiation. Paused weeks preserve unfinished work, and the program deadline ends a sale not yet held. Ending planning authority prevents a new award but does not erase an awarded lease. A permit is not production.

An accepted bid creates an identified bonus obligation with due and received turns. `NationBooks.leaseBonusReceiptIds?` records receipts already booked. The national fiscal phase consumes a dated unbooked receipt once through `other` revenue and the existing debt calculation. It does not credit presidential discretionary authority. Neither a sale nor an exploration permit directly changes national imports.


### Bounded federal purchasing and foreign-property blocks (2026-10-12)

`Campaign.cleanPowerPurchases?` stores `CleanPowerPurchase` records through the `cleanPowerPurchase` effect. Each names the exact order record, the `federal-property-portfolio` buyer, its fixed USD-million obligation, total and delivered MWh, contract turn, `lastDeliveryTurn`, and completion status/date. Re-running the phase in the same saved turn cannot add another installment. The modeled portfolio contract is 700,000 MWh over 104 weekly installments with a rounded $94 million obligation; its scale and retail-price proxy are documented in source, not represented as an actual awarded clean-energy bid. `renewableSupplyMwh` derives weekly procurement headroom from the live national renewable share and a stated EIA generation baseline; its one-percent headroom is a game abstraction. A supply shortfall delays delivery. Ending the purchasing rule bars a new award but does not cancel existing contractual delivery or refund its obligation. A signature alone cannot fulfill the energy pledge; its delivered-purchase test also requires the existing import-reduction outcome. Old saves without this array have no invented purchase.

`Campaign.blockedAssets?` stores a clearly modeled U.S.-custodied foreign-government account with owner, exact order/emergency IDs, uniquely linked `tradeMeasureId`, blocked/released status and dated reason. It is available only with measured bilateral import exposure and a matching active external emergency; the account is a game representation of settlement exposure, not evidence of an actual bank account or balance. Duplicate active blocks of that target are refused. `blockedAsset` uses the ordinary effect gate. Its linked `asset-restriction` measure supplies the actual dealings restriction through the existing trade-volume consumer. Rescission lifts that identified measure immediately; judicial invalidation or emergency expiry releases it through `blockedAssetPhase`. Ownership never transfers, no money enters Treasury, and unrelated trade measures remain.

The `ports-of-entry` order subject explicitly records `scope: all-federal-ports`; it grants no interior-policing authority. The `entry-class` subject freezes the foreign nation and the observed security pursuit supporting the first-entry proclamation. The service producer ties an actual consular refusal to that selected class and order occurrence.


### Prospective order authority and recorded contract delivery (2026-10-12)

`ExecutiveOrderRecord.defenseContract?` identifies a modeled supplier, `munitionClass: "precision-fires"`, the existing order's `initialIndex`, due date and `provenance: "planning value"`. `MunitionStock.onOrder[].priorityContractId?` links that existing replenishment entry to the direction. Stock units are bounded indices, not physical counts or dollar prices. Signing requires a backlog and current military allocation, outlay and appropriation. It adds no purchase price or immediate readiness gain. `MunitionsState.lastProcessedTurn?` guards repeated weekly processing. Priority affects only the tagged order while prospective authority and applicable relief permit it; the tagged residual cannot appear automatically at its due date. Existing stock and backlog survive loss of priority.
