`CleanPowerPurchase.paidUsdMillions?` records supplier payments in USD millions for delivered electricity, separately from the original obligation. `paymentTrackingStartMwh?` is the delivered-MWh baseline for that cash record. New awards start both at zero. A legacy record lacking the fields starts tracking at its already-delivered amount on the next delivery: earlier payments remain unknown, never reconstructed as fact. `FiscalStatement.cleanPowerPaymentsRecorded` is the cumulative recorded-payment memo, not an annualized outlay component; it must not be added again to debt or discretionary spending.

### Court scheduling and government litigation conduct (2026-10-12)

`CourtProceeding.calendar?` stores `termYear`, `argumentTurn`, `opinionTurn`, `opinionTargetTurn` and `status` (`scheduled | argued | carried`). These are campaign turns. The late-June opinion target is a scheduling assumption, not the end of the legal term or an automatic disposal deadline. A missing quorum carries the proceeding; emergency applications retain their separate clock. The frozen `reviewDecision` and stay factors retain the standing and adjustment used when the court decided that application, rather than reading today's record into an old decision.

`Judiciary.solicitorGeneral?` is an optional `{ standing, events }` record. Standing is a bounded 15–85 game assessment with a neutral default of 50, not an observed court statistic. The `solicitorGeneral` effect records the proceeding, turn, event kind, change and account. Ordinary grant, denial and merits outcomes are dated facts with zero standing change. Persisting in a position the lower court found forbidden, or abandoning that position, supplies the modeled conduct signal. This is a narrow overreach/correction model, not a complete evaluation of counsel. Its adjustment affects review and stay consideration only; ordinary screens show a qualitative assessment and dated reasons. Legacy absence preserves the absence of prior events.

### Lower courts and uncertain border incidents (2026-10-12)

`CourtProceeding.lowerForum?` freezes the selected Article III district forum, circuit and dated aggregate seat counts when an order challenge is filed. The source has 91 district forums and three shared-judgeship groups; shared seats are not counted twice in national totals. `lower.modeledStrikeChance?` and `lower.draw?` retain diagnostic inputs, not player-facing calibrated probabilities. `circuitPanel?` records the assignment turn, three seat/person pairs and case-specific prior-government-advice recusals with their turn and reason; it remains on the proceeding after judgment. `circuit?` freezes the appellate court, ruling turn, holding and participating votes by stable seat and person IDs. Completed `CourtCase.procedure` copies the lower and circuit rulings, but not the panel assignment; absence on an old proceeding or opinion does not manufacture a historical panel.

The 179 circuit offices resolve through the existing institution/office readers outside the executive agency catalog. An occupied anonymous seat is not a vacancy merely because `leader` is absent. `InstitutionRuntime.lowerBenchSeat?` records circuit, source date and available appointing-party metadata; generated identities and their age/service assumptions are fictional, not identities inferred from source party counts. The annual Article III vacancy phase assesses occupied Supreme and circuit seats once per calendar year, including anonymous circuit incumbents, and stores `judicialAssessmentYear` and service provenance. Death or pension-eligible retirement vacates the seat; a named former holder cannot be rematerialized as a successor. Panel assignment materializes only eligible occupants through the normal effect fold. Vacant, dead, displaced and case-recused holders cannot sit; if one assigned judge leaves, the other two can decide unanimously, while a split seeks a third judge and leaves the appeal pending. Circuit seats do not enter executive department delivery or budget-adequacy averages. Other recusal grounds and broader lower-court lifecycle details remain outside this model.

`Campaign.blockades?` (`Record<id, Blockade>`, `blockades.ts`) is every blockade any government has laid, lifted ones kept. `Blockade = { id, imposer, target, startedTurn, cut, conflictId, commitmentId?, obligationId? }`, keyed `blockade:<imposer>:<target>:<turn>`. `cut` (0..`BLOCKADE_MAX_CUT` 0.9) is the share of the target's seaborne trade stopped while it stands: `(BLOCKADE_BASE_CUT 0.5 + BLOCKADE_STRAIT_CUT 0.25 × chokepointExposure(target)) × forceScale`, planning values. No field records the lift: a blockade is in force while its war is active, its deployment commitment (where the imposer's books carry one) is active and its War Powers introduction (where the imposer has a Congress) is not terminated, so a recall, a war's end or `terminateForce` lifts it the same week. Written by the `blockade` effect kind; absent on older saves, which read as none laid. Nothing writes the target's `stats.trade`.

`Campaign.npcTempo?` (`TempoEntry[]`, `npc-tempo.ts`) is other governments' rationed acts, one entry per act per family: `TempoEntry = { family, key, actor, turn, keepUntil }`. `family` is a `TempoFamily.id` (`npc-force`, `strait-closure`; a family is declared by the module that owns the act as `{ id, perTerm, cooldownWeeks, noun }`); `key` is what rests after the act (`pairKey(a, b)`, the two ids sorted and joined by `|`, or a ChokepointId); `keepUntil` is `turn + max(208, cooldownWeeks)`, after which the entry can refuse nothing and is dropped the next time the ledger is written. Written by the `npcTempo` effect kind (`{ entries }`, appended); absent on older saves, which read as no acts made.

`Campaign.hullLosses?` (`HullLoss[]`, `hull-losses.ts`) is the President's hulls lost in wars, oldest first: `HullLoss = { id, nation, unitType, hull?, name?, conflictId, turn, killed }`, keyed `hull-loss:<conflictId>:<turn>`; `hull` and `name` are the register's (`US_HULLS`) where the class still has an unlost name. Written by the `hullLoss` effect kind (`{ set }`, appended) in the same war-week packet that takes the hull off the deployment (`source.count` less one, wound down at zero) and off `stats.military.orderOfBattle.navy.units`, files `killed` as a `populationToll` and adds it to the war's casualty band on the President's side. Absent on older saves, which read as none lost.

`Conflict.civilianDeaths?` (number, `war-attrition.ts#civilianDeaths`) is the civilians killed in the defender's country so far, the central estimate, added every war week at `CIVILIANS_PER_SOLDIER_KILLED` (0.3) of the defending side's soldiers killed that week and filed against the defender's population as a `populationToll`. Absent until a week is counted and on older saves, which read as none.

`Campaign.borderIncidents?` stores bilateral incidents separately from deliberate coercive acts. Each `BorderIncident` names a sorted neighboring pair, opening turn, revision and causal event reference; there is no inferred initiator. Its `pressure` tuple freezes each directed edge’s pre-incident tension/threat and exact clamped additions. Later assessment relief cannot exceed those additions or reduce an edge below its pre-incident reading. The `borderIncident` effect requires the expected prior revision. A later assessment records its turn, `likely-inadvertent | undetermined` finding, qualitative confidence and the evidence obtained. An absent assessment means the cause has not been assessed. Missing legacy incident state creates no prior incident or prior knowledge. The world-event phase produces these records and public feed/history entries; collection can quote a dated assessment without converting uncertainty into blame. Frequency and evidence probabilities are explicit provisional simulation assumptions.

`OversightMatter.surveillanceIncidentId?` pins the exact persisted interception examined by a Defense inspector investigation. The inspector producer assigns it when opening that investigation; the reporting transition exposes only that matching incident. An unrelated Defense audit or an older different interception cannot expose the current record. Save/load preserves the optional identity; its absence on older matters does not invent an investigative subject.

## Additive story and scenario-accounting fields (2026-10-14)

`CaseRaise` and `PresidentialCase` may carry `story: StoryBinding`, containing `scenario`, optional `family`, optional `place: {state, city?}`, and `subjects: StorySubject[]`. Each subject retains `key`, stable `id`, `kind`, `name`, `description`, `nation`, optional `state/city/person/institution`, and `originCase/originScenario/originTurn`. The existing case registry persists the binding. Old cases without it remain unbound; no identity is reverse-inferred from prose.

`ConversationScript` optionally carries `subjects` and `subjectCauseIds`; its existing `gatedOn` now receives true authored flag gates. `EffectPacket` and `ActionRecord` optionally retain `subjects` and `accounting: ScenarioAccounting[]`. Accounting stores `estimated`, `booked`, `variance`, `source`, `method` (`arithmetic` or `band`) and `calibration: planning`, in USD millions. `choiceBorrowing` may carry estimate metadata (`key/source/method/spread`) on a frozen option. The commit path settles that metadata into an untagged booked effect; projection keeps the estimate. Existing numeric debt precision is unchanged.

These fields are additive on the current development save format, absent on older cases/entries. Existing saves remain readable; absence means unknown identity or no historical estimate, never a reconstructed fact. Authored `ScenarioDef.subjects` is optional; registered `entities` also become durable subjects. `financial.basis.components` adds separately sourced cost components; `coverage` names the accounting boundary and is required by the basis audit.

`WorldDataset.settlements?` adds sourced US locality context (`Settlement`): a branded id, nation/state, name, coordinates, source population and source version, with optional evidenced `commercialPort` or `roadBorderCrossing`. The validator rejects duplicate ids, unknown states, non-US references and unflagged points above the small-place tier. `scripts/bake-cities.mjs` derives the table from the existing Natural Earth v5.1.2 input; economic cities remain unchanged. The 100,000 ceiling is a model size tier, not a legal town definition or proof of rural land use. Port Newark and International Falls have separately recorded primary-source capability evidence. No facility throughput, crop suitability, municipal budget or employment is inferred.

`ScenarioDef.locus` accepts `settlement: small | commercial-port | road-border` and optional `settlementId`. The resolved locus, story place and locale stamp retain that id. An explicit unsupported locality requirement fails closed. A national condition with no state cannot supply a local place. `StorySubject.kind` includes `place`; locality ids support the same Record subject history as other identities.

Subject slots also accept `mayor` and `member` (House/Senate, state/nation scope). Person/institution subject ids use the existing native identity across unrelated cases; authored token aliases remain distinct. `PersonRuntime.municipalCity?` associates a lazily created fictional mayor with an existing economic city. The existing person-create effect persists the official at case raise. No municipal elections or service-capacity model is introduced. Direct official slots select the current holder; `inherit` explicitly preserves the original person. Domestic `letter`/`readout` transmissions require a declared governor/mayor/member or inherited person slot through `domesticSubject`, retaining the cabinet/narrator/player direct-speaker boundary.

The canonical dataset `Place`/`PlaceTarget` resolver accepts an optional `settlement` and a `settlement` place level. `settlementPlace` returns the sourced point and state-qualified name. An explicit unknown or contradictory settlement target returns no point instead of falling back to a metro. Record event placement reads that same point from the causal locale stamp, retaining the state camera while pinning the actual locality.

### Guard support coordination monetary contract

The historical `requestGuardFederalization` API records governor support coordination and an answered flag. Its consenting metric packet charges `GUARD_SUPPORT_COORDINATION_USD_MILLIONS = 10` in the treasury's USD-million unit. It does not create a force deployment or change command status. The API name is retained; it is not evidence of a modeled legal federalization pathway. Save shapes are unchanged.

### Domestic-force availability boundary (October 15, 2026)

The existing locale and home executors refuse generic federal agent surges, Guard federalization and active-duty domestic troop deployment. A declaration, unrest or a foreign invasion flag does not supply the missing mission, jurisdiction, findings, command status or statutory procedures. No new saved fields are introduced. Governor assent requires an actual governor record. State-controlled support requests remain modeled separately; historical records and their coercion consequences remain readable. The user interface uses the same executor availability and explains the boundary rather than asserting blanket lawfulness.
